BOOK XLVIII

Bel and the Dragon

❖

14

1 King Astyages was gathered to his fathers, and Cyrus the Persian received his kingdom. 2 Daniel lived with the king, and was honored above all his friends. 3 Now the Babylonians had an idol called Bel, and every day twelve great measures of fine flour, forty sheep, and six firkins of wine were spent on it. 4 The king honored it and went daily to worship it; but Daniel worshiped his own God1. The king said to him, “Why don’t you worship Bel?” 5 He said, “Because I may not honor idols made with hands, but only the living God2, who has created3 the sky and the earth4, and has sovereignty over all flesh.”5 6 Then the king said to him, “Don’t you think that Bel is a living god? Don’t you see how much he eats and drinks every day?”6 7 Then Daniel laughed, and said, “O king, don’t be deceived; for this is just clay inside, and brass outside, and never ate or drank anything.” 8 So the king was angry, and called for his priests, and said to them, “If you don’t tell me who this is who devours these expenses, you shall die. 9 But if you can show me that Bel devours them, then Daniel shall die; for he has spoken blasphemy against Bel.” Daniel said to the king, “Let it be according to your word.” 10 Now there were seventy priests of Bel, besides their wives and children. The king went with Daniel into Bel’s temple. 11 So Bel’s priests said, “Behold, we will leave; but you, O king, set out the food, and mix the wine and set it out, shut the door securely, and seal it with your own signet. 12 When you come in the morning, if you don’t find that Bel has eaten everything, we will suffer death, or else Daniel, who speaks falsely against us.” 13 They weren’t concerned, for under the table they had made a secret entrance, by which they entered in continually, and consumed those things. 14 It happened, when they had gone out, the king set the food before Bel. Now Daniel had commanded his servants to bring ashes, and they scattered them all over the temple in the presence of the king alone. Then they went out, shut the door, sealed it with the king’s signet, and so departed. 15 Now in the night, the priests came with their wives and children, as they usually did, and ate and drank it all. 16 In the morning, the king arose, and Daniel with him. 17 The king said, “Daniel, are the seals whole?” He said, “Yes, O king, they are whole.” 18 And as soon as he had opened the door, the king looked at the table, and cried with a loud voice, “You are great, O Bel, and with you is no deceit at all!” 19 Then Daniel laughed, and held the king that he should not go in, and said, “Behold now the pavement, and mark well whose footsteps these are.” 20 The king said, “I see the footsteps of men, women, and children.” Then the king was angry, 21 and took the priests with their wives and children, who showed him the secret doors, where they came in and consumed the things that were on the table. 22 Therefore the king killed them, and delivered Bel into Daniel’s power, who overthrew it and its temple. 23 In that same place there was a great dragon which the people of Babylon worshiped. 24 The king said to Daniel, “Will you also say that this is of brass? Behold, he lives, eats and drinks. You can’t say that he is no living god7. Therefore worship him.” 25 Then Daniel said, “I will worship the Lord8 my God9; for he is a living God. 26 But allow me, O king, and I will kill this dragon without sword or staff.” The king said, “I allow you.” 27 Then Daniel took pitch, fat, and hair, and melted them together, and made lumps of them. He put these in the dragon’s mouth, so the dragon ate and burst apart. Daniel said, “Behold, these are the gods you all worship.” 28 When the people of Babylon heard that, they took great indignation, and conspired against the king, saying, “The king has become a Jew. He has pulled down Bel, slain the dragon, and put the priests to the sword.” 29 So they came to the king, and said, “Deliver Daniel to us, or else we will destroy you and your house.” 30 Now when the king saw that they trapped him, being constrained, the king delivered Daniel to them. 31 They cast him into the lion’s den, where he was six days. 32 There were seven lions in the den, and they had been giving them two carcasses and two sheep every day, which then were not given to them, intending that they would devour Daniel. 33 Now there was in Jewry the prophet Habakkuk, who had made stew, and had broken bread into a bowl. He was going into the field to bring it to the reapers. 34 But the angel of the Lord10 said to Habakkuk, “Go carry the dinner that you have into Babylon to Daniel, in the lions’ den.” 35 Habakkuk said, “Lord11, I never saw Babylon. I don’t know where the den is.” 36 Then the angel of the Lord12 took him by the crown, and lifted him up by the hair of his head, and with the blast of his breath set him in Babylon over the den.13 37 Habakkuk cried, saying, “O Daniel, Daniel, take the dinner which God14 has sent you.” 38 Daniel said, “You have remembered me, O God15! You haven’t forsaken those who love16 you!” 39 So Daniel arose and ate; and the angel of God set Habakkuk in his own place again immediately.17 40 On the seventh day, the king came to mourn for Daniel. When he came to the den, he looked in, and, behold, Daniel was sitting. 41 Then the king cried with a loud voice, saying, “Great are you, O Lord18, you God19 of Daniel, and there is none other beside you!” 42 So he drew him out, and cast those that were the cause of his destruction into the den; and they were devoured in a moment before his face.

BOOK XLIX

The Book of Hosea

❖

1

1 Yahweh’s word that came to Hosea the son of Beeri, in the days of Uzziah, Jotham, Ahaz, and Hezekiah, kings of Judah, and in the days of Jeroboam the son of Joash, king of Israel.123 ✦2 When Yahweh spoke at first by Hosea, Yahweh said to Hosea, “Go, take for yourself a wife of prostitution and children of unfaithfulness; for the land4 commits great adultery, forsaking Yahweh.”56 ✦3 So he went and took Gomer the daughter of Diblaim; and she conceived, and bore him a son.7 ✦4 Yahweh said to him, “Call his name Jezreel, for yet a little while, and I will avenge the blood8 of Jezreel on the house of Jehu, and will cause the kingdom of the house of Israel to cease.910 ✦5 It will happen in that day that I will break the bow of Israel in the valley of Jezreel.”11 ✦6 She conceived again, and bore a daughter. Then he said to him, “Call her name Lo-Ruhamah, for I will no longer have mercy12 on the house of Israel, that I should in any way pardon them.1314 ✦7 But I will have mercy15 on the house of Judah, and will save them by Yahweh their God16, and will not save them by bow, sword, battle, horses, or horsemen.”1718 ✦8 Now when she had weaned Lo-Ruhamah, she conceived, and bore a son.19 ✦9 He said, “Call his name Lo-Ammi, for you are not my people, and I will not be yours.2021 ✦10 Yet the number of the children of Israel will be as the sand of the sea, which can’t be measured or counted; and it will come to pass that, in the place where it was said to them, ‘You are not my people,’ they will be called ‘sons of the living God22.’2324 11 The children of Judah and the children of Israel will be gathered together, and they will appoint themselves one head, and will go up from the land25; for great will be the day of Jezreel.

26✦

2

11 2 “Say to your brothers, ‘My people!’ and to your sisters, ‘My loved one!’

Contend with your mother! Contend, for she is not my wife, neither am I her husband; and let her put away her prostitution from her face, and her adulteries from between her breasts; 3 lest I strip her naked2, and make her bare as in the day that she was born, and make her like a wilderness, and set her like a dry land3, and kill her with thirst. ✦4 Indeed, on her children I will have no mercy4, for they are children of unfaithfulness. 5 For their mother has played the prostitute. She who conceived them has done shamefully; for she said, ‘I will go after my lovers, who give me my bread and my water5, my wool and my flax, my oil and my drink.’6 ✦6 Therefore behold, I will hedge up your way with thorns, and I will build a wall against her, that she can’t find her way.7 ✦7 She will follow after her lovers, but she won’t overtake them; and she will seek them, but won’t find them. Then she will say, ‘I will go and return to my first husband, for then it was better with me than now.’ ✦8 For she didn’t know that I gave her the grain, the new wine, and the oil, and multiplied to her silver and gold, which they used for Baal.8 ✦9 Therefore I will take back my grain in its time, and my new wine in its season, and will pluck away my wool and my flax which should have covered her nakedness.9 ✦10 Now I will uncover her lewdness in the sight of her lovers, and no one will deliver her out of my hand. 11 I will also cause all her celebrations to cease: her feasts, her new moons, her Sabbaths, and all her solemn assemblies. ✦12 I will lay waste her vines and her fig trees, about which she has said, ‘These are my wages that my lovers have given me,’ and I will make them a forest, and the animals of the field shall eat them. ✦13 I will visit on her the days of the Baals, to which she burned incense when she decked herself with her earrings and her jewels, and went after her lovers and forgot me,” says Yahweh.1011 ✦14 “Therefore behold, I will allure her, and bring her into the wilderness, and speak tenderly to her.12 ✦15 I will give her vineyards from there, and the valley of Achor for a door of hope; and she will respond there as in the days of her youth, and as in the day when she came up out of the land13 of Egypt.14 ✦16 It will be in that day,” says Yahweh, “that you will call me ‘my husband,’ and no longer call me ‘my master.’1516 17 For I will take away the names of the Baals out of her mouth, and they will no longer be mentioned by name.17 18 In that day I will make a covenant18 for them with the animals of the field, and with the birds of the sky, and with the creeping things of the ground19. I will break the bow, the sword, and the battle out of the land, and will make them lie down safely.202122 ✦19 I will betroth you to me forever. Yes, I will betroth you to me in righteousness23, in justice, in loving kindness, and in compassion.2425 ✦20 I will even betroth you to me in faithfulness; and you shall know Yahweh.2627 ✦21 It will happen in that day, that I will respond,” says Yahweh. “I will respond to the heavens28, and they will respond to the earth29;3031 22 and the earth32 will respond to the grain, and the new wine, and the oil; and they will respond to Jezreel.33 ✦23 I will sow her to me in the earth34; and I will have mercy35 on her who had not obtained mercy; and I will tell those who were not my people, ‘You are my people;’ and they will say, ‘You are My God36!’”

373839✦

3

1 Yahweh said to me, “Go again, love1 a woman2 loved by another, and an adulteress, even as Yahweh loves the children of Israel, though they turn to other gods, and love cakes of raisins.”34 ✦2 So I bought her for myself for fifteen pieces of silver and a homer and a half of barley.5 ✦3 I said to her, “You shall stay with me man6y days. You shall not play the prostitute, and you shall not be with any other man. I will also be so toward you.”7 4 For the children of Israel shall live many days without king, without prince, without sacrifice, without sacred stone, and without ephod or idols.89 ✦5 Afterward the children of Israel shall return and seek Yahweh their God10, and David their king, and shall come with trembling to Yahweh and to his blessings in the last days.

111213✦

4

1 Hear Yahweh’s word, you children of Israel, for Yahweh has a charge against the inhabitants of the land1: “Indeed there is no truth, nor goodness, nor knowledge of God2 in the land.345 ✦2 There is cursing, lying, murder, stealing, and committing adultery; they break boundaries, and blood6shed causes bloodshed.7 ✦3 Therefore the land8 will mourn, and everyone who dwells in it will waste away, with all living things in her, even the animals of the field and the birds of the sky; yes, the fish of the sea also die.910 ✦4 “Yet let no man11 bring a charge, neither let any man accuse; for your people are like those who bring charges against a priest.12 5 You will stumble in the day, and the prophet will also stumble with you in the night; and I will destroy your mother. 6 My people are destroyed for lack of knowledge. Because you have rejected knowledge, I will also reject you, that you may be no priest to me. Because you have forgotten your God13’s law, I will also forget your children.141516 ✦7 As they were multiplied, so they sinned against me. I will change their glory17 into shame.18 ✦8 They feed on the sin19 of my people, and set their heart20 on their iniquity. ✦9 It will be like people, like priest; and I will punish them for their ways, and will repay them for their deeds.21 ✦10 They will eat, and not have enough. They will play the prostitute, and will not increase; because they have abandoned listening to Yahweh.22 11 Prostitution, wine, and new wine take away understanding.23 ✦12 My people consult with their wooden idol, and answer to a stick of wood. Indeed the spirit24 of prostitution has led them astray, and they have been unfaithful to their God25.26 ✦13 They sacrifice on the tops of the mountains, and burn incense on the hills, under oaks, poplars, and terebinths, because its shade is good27. Therefore your daughters play the prostitute, and your brides commit adultery.28 ✦14 I will not punish your daughters when they play the prostitute, nor your brides when they commit adultery; because the men consort with prostitutes, and they sacrifice with the shrine prostitutes; so the people without understanding will come to ruin.29 15 “Though you, Israel, play the prostitute, yet don’t let Judah offend; and don’t come to Gilgal, neither go up to Beth Aven, nor swear, ‘As Yahweh lives.’3031 16 For Israel has behaved extremely stubbornly, like a stubborn heifer. Then how will Yahweh feed them like a lamb in a meadow?3233 ✦17 Ephraim is joined to idols. Leave him alone!34 ✦18 Their drink has become sour. They play the prostitute continually. Her rulers dearly love35 their shameful way.36 19 The wind37 has wrapped her up in its wings; and they shall be disappointed because of their sacrifices.

38

5

1 “Listen to this, you priests! Listen, house of Israel, and give ear, house of the king! For the judgment is against you; for you have been a snare at Mizpah, and a net spread on Tabor. 2 The rebels are deep in slaughter, but I discipline all of them. 3 I know Ephraim, and Israel is not hidden from me; for now, Ephraim, you have played the prostitute. Israel is defiled. 4 Their deeds won’t allow them to turn to their God1, for the spirit2 of prostitution is within them, and they don’t know Yahweh.3 5 The pride of Israel testifies to his face. Therefore Israel and Ephraim will stumble in their iniquity. Judah also will stumble with them. ✦6 They will go with their flocks and with their herds to seek Yahweh, but they won’t find him. He has withdrawn himself from them.45 7 They are unfaithful to Yahweh; for they have borne illegitimate children. Now the new moon will devour them with their fields.6 8 “Blow the cornet in Gibeah, and the trumpet in Ramah! Sound a battle cry at Beth Aven, behind you, Benjamin!7 ✦9 Ephraim will become a desolation in the day of rebuke. Among the tribes of Israel, I have made known that which will surely be. ✦10 The princes of Judah are like those who remove a landmark. I will pour out my wrath on them like water8.9 ✦11 Ephraim is oppressed, he is crushed in judgment, because he is intent in his pursuit of idols. 12 Therefore I am to Ephraim like a moth, and to the house of Judah like rottenness.10 ✦13 “When Ephraim saw his sickness, and Judah his wound, then Ephraim went to Assyria, and sent to King Jareb: but he is not able to heal you, neither will he cure you of your wound.11 ✦14 For I will be to Ephraim like a lion, and like a young lion to the house of Judah. I myself will tear in pieces and go away. I will carry off, and there will be no one to deliver.12 ✦15 I will go and return to my place, until they acknowledge their offense, and seek my face. In their affliction they will seek me earnestly.”

✦

6

1 “Come! Let’s return to Yahweh; for he has torn us to pieces, and he will heal us; he has injured us, and he will bind up our wounds.123 ✦2 After two days he will revive us. On the third day he will raise us up, and we will live before him.4 ✦3 Let’s acknowledge Yahweh. Let’s press on to know Yahweh. As surely as the sun rises, Yahweh will appear. He will come to us like the rain, like the spring rain that waters the earth5.”678 ✦4 “Ephraim, what shall I do to you? Judah, what shall I do to you? For your love is like a morning cloud, and like the dew that disappears early.9 ✦5 Therefore I have cut them to pieces with the prophets; I killed them with the words of my mouth. Your judgments are like a flash of light10ning. 6 For I desire mercy11, and not sacrifice; and the knowledge of God12 more than burnt offerings.131415 ✦7 But they, like Adam, have broken the covenant16. They were unfaithful to me there.17 8 Gilead is a city of those who work iniquity; it is stained with blood18.19 ✦9 As gangs of robbers wait to ambush a man20, so the company of priests murder on the path toward Shechem, committing shameful crimes.21 ✦10 In the house of Israel I have seen a horrible thing. There is prostitution in Ephraim. Israel is defiled. 11 “ Also, Judah, there is a harvest appointed for you, when I restore the fortunes of my people.

✦

7

1 When I would heal Israel, then the iniquity of Ephraim is uncovered, also the wickedness of Samaria; for they commit falsehood, and the thief enters in, and the gang of robbers ravages outside.1 ✦2 They don’t consider in their hearts that I remember all their wickedness. Now their own deeds have engulfed them. They are before my face. 3 They make the king glad with their wickedness, and the princes with their lies. ✦4 They are all adulterers. They are burning like an oven that the baker stops stirring, from the kneading of the dough, until it is leavened.2 ✦5 On the day of our king, the princes made themselves sick with the heat of wine. He joined his hand with mockers.3 ✦6 For they have prepared their heart4 like an oven, while they lie in wait. Their anger smolders all night. In the morning it burns as a flaming fire.5 ✦7 They are all hot as an oven, and devour their judges. All their kings have fallen. There is no one among them who calls to me.6 ✦8 Ephraim mixes himself among the nations. Ephraim is a pancake not turned over.7 9 Strangers have devoured his strength, and he doesn’t realize it. Indeed, gray hairs are here and there on him, and he doesn’t realize it. ✦10 The pride of Israel testifies to his face; yet they haven’t returned to Yahweh their God8, nor sought him, for all this.9 ✦11 “Ephraim is like an easily deceived dove, without understanding. They call to Egypt. They go to Assyria.1011 ✦12 When they go, I will spread my net on them. I will bring them down like the birds of the sky. I will chastise them, as their congregation has heard.12 13 Woe to them! For they have wandered from me. Destruction to them! For they have trespassed against me. Though I would redeem them, yet they have spoken lies against me. 14 They haven’t cried to me with their heart13, but they howl on their beds. They assemble themselves for grain and new wine. They turn away from me. ✦15 Though I have taught and strengthened their arms, yet they plot evil against me. 16 They return, but not to the Most High. They are like a faulty bow. Their princes will fall by the sword for the rage of their tongue. This will be their derision in the land14 of Egypt.

8

1 “Put the trumpet to your lips! Something like an eagle is over Yahweh’s house, because they have broken my covenant1 and rebelled against my law.23 ✦2 They cry to me, ‘My God4, we, Israel, acknowledge you!’5 3 Israel has cast off that which is good6. The enemy will pursue him. 4 They have set up kings, but not by me. They have made princes, and I didn’t approve. Of their silver and their gold they have made themselves idols, that they may be cut off.7 5 Let Samaria throw out his calf idol! My anger burns against them! How long will it be until they are capable of purity?8 ✦6 For this is even from Israel! The workman made it, and it is no God9; indeed, the calf of Samaria shall be broken in pieces.10 ✦7 For they sow the wind11, and they will reap the whirlwind. He has no standing grain. The stalk will yield no head. If it does yield, strangers will swallow it up.12 ✦8 Israel is swallowed up. Now they are among the nations like a worthless thing. ✦9 For they have gone up to Assyria, like a wild donkey wandering alone. Ephraim has hired lovers for himself.13 ✦10 But although they sold themselves among the nations, I will now gather them; and they begin to waste away because of the oppression of the king of mighty ones. 11 Because Ephraim has multiplied altars for sin14ning, they became for him altars for sinning. ✦12 I wrote for him the many things of my law, but they were regarded as a strange thing.15 13 As for the sacrifices of my offerings, they sacrifice meat and eat it, but Yahweh doesn’t accept them. Now he will remember their iniquity, and punish their sins. They will return to Egypt.16 ✦14 For Israel has forgotten his Maker and built palaces; and Judah has multiplied fortified cities; but I will send a fire on his cities, and it will devour its fortresses.”

17✦

9

1 Don’t rejoice, Israel, to jubilation like the nations; for you were unfaithful to your God1. You love the wages of a prostitute at every grain threshing floor. ✦2 The threshing floor and the wine press won’t feed them, and the new wine will fail her.2 ✦3 They won’t dwell in Yahweh’s land3; but Ephraim will return to Egypt, and they will eat unclean food in Assyria.45 ✦4 They won’t pour out wine offerings to Yahweh, neither will they be pleasing to him. Their sacrifices will be to them like the bread of mourners; all who eat of it will be polluted; for their bread will be for their appetite. It will not come into Yahweh’s house.678 ✦5 What will you do in the day of solemn assembly, and in the day of the feast of Yahweh?9 ✦6 For, behold, when they flee destruction, Egypt will gather them up. Memphis will bury them. Nettles will possess their pleasant things of silver. Thorns will be in their tents.10 7 The days of visitation have come. The days of reckoning have come. Israel will consider the prophet to be a fool, and the man11 who is inspired to be insane, because of the abundance of your sins, and because your hostility is great. 8 A prophet watches over Ephraim with my God12. A fowler’s snare is on all of his paths, and hostility in the house of his God. 9 They have deeply corrupted themselves, as in the days of Gibeah. He will remember their iniquity. He will punish them for their sins.13 10 I found Israel like grapes in the wilderness. I saw your fathers as the first ripe in the fig tree at its first season; but they came to Baal Peor, and consecrated themselves to the shameful thing, and became abominable like that which they loved.14 ✦11 As for Ephraim, their glory15 will fly away like a bird. There will be no birth, no one with child, and no conception. ✦12 Though they bring up their children, yet I will bereave them, so that not a man16 shall be left. Indeed, woe also to them when I depart from them!17 13 I have seen Ephraim, like Tyre, planted in a pleasant place; but Ephraim will bring out his children to the murderer.18 14 Give them—Yahweh what will you give? Give them a miscarrying womb and dry breasts.1920 15 “All their wickedness is in Gilgal; for there I hated them. Because of the wickedness of their deeds, I will drive them out of my house! I will love21 them no more. All their princes are rebels.22 16 Ephraim is struck. Their root has dried up. They will bear no fruit. Even though they give birth, yet I will kill the beloved ones of their womb.” ✦17 My God23 will cast them away, because they didn’t listen to him; and they will be wanderers among the nations.

10

1 Israel is a luxuriant vine that produces his fruit. According to the abundance of his fruit he has multiplied his altars. As their land1 has prospered, they have adorned their sacred stones.23 ✦2 Their heart4 is divided. Now they will be found guilty. He will demolish their altars. He will destroy their sacred stones.5 ✦3 Surely now they will say, “We have no king; for we don’t fear Yahweh; and the king, what can he do for us?”67 4 They make promises, swearing falsely in making covenant8s. Therefore judgment springs up like poisonous weeds in the furrows of the field.9 ✦5 The inhabitants of Samaria will be in terror for the calves of Beth Aven, for its people will mourn over it, along with its priests who rejoiced over it, for its glory10, because it has departed from it.1112 ✦6 It also will be carried to Assyria for a present to a great king. Ephraim will receive shame, and Israel will be ashamed of his own counsel.13 ✦7 Samaria and her king float away like a twig on the water14.15 8 The high places also of Aven, the sin16 of Israel, will be destroyed. The thorn and the thistle will come up on their altars. They will tell the mountains, “Cover us!” and the hills, “Fall on us!”17 ✦9 “Israel, you have sinned from the days of Gibeah. There they remained. The battle against the children of iniquity doesn’t overtake them in Gibeah.18 ✦10 When it is my desire, I will chastise them; and the nations will be gathered against them when they are bound to their two transgressions. 11 Ephraim is a trained heifer that loves to thresh, so I will put a yoke on her beautiful neck. I will set a rider on Ephraim. Judah will plow. Jacob will break his clods.19 ✦12 Sow to yourselves in righteousness20, reap according to kindness. Break up your fallow ground21, for it is time to seek Yahweh, until he comes and rains righteousness on you.2223242526 ✦13 You have plowed wickedness. You have reaped iniquity. You have eaten the fruit of lies, for you trusted in your way, in the multitude of your mighty men.27 ✦14 Therefore a battle roar will arise among your people, and all your fortresses will be destroyed, as Shalman destroyed Beth Arbel in the day of battle. The mother was dashed in pieces with her children.28 ✦15 So Bethel will do to you because of your great wickedness. At daybreak the king of Israel will be destroyed.

11

1 “When Israel was a child, then I loved him, and called my son out of Egypt.

1✦2 They called to them, so they went from them. They sacrificed to the Baals, and burned incense to engraved images.2 ✦3 Yet I taught Ephraim to walk. I took them by their arms, but they didn’t know that I healed them.3 4 I drew them with cords of a man4, with ties of love5; and I was to them like those who lift up the yoke on their necks; and I bent down to him and I fed him.6 ✦5 “They won’t return into the land7 of Egypt; but the Assyrian will be their king, because they refused to repent.8 ✦6 The sword will fall on their cities, and will destroy the bars of their gates, and will put an end to their plans.9 7 My people are determined to turn from me. Though they call to the Most High, he certainly won’t exalt them. ✦8 “How can I give you up, Ephraim? How can I hand you over, Israel? How can I make you like Admah? How can I make you like Zeboiim? My heart10 is turned within me, my compassion is aroused.11 9 I will not execute the fierceness of my anger. I will not return to destroy Ephraim, for I am God12, and not man13—the Holy14 One among you. I will not come in wrath.1516 10 They will walk after Yahweh, who will roar like a lion; for he will roar, and the children will come trembling from the west.1718 11 They will come trembling like a bird out of Egypt, and like a dove out of the land19 of Assyria; and I will settle them in their houses,” says Yahweh.2021 ✦12 Ephraim surrounds me with falsehood, and the house of Israel with deceit. Judah still strays from God22, and is unfaithful to the Holy One.

23✦

12

1 Ephraim feeds on wind1, and chases the east wind. He continually multiplies lies and desolation. They make a covenant2 with Assyria, and oil is carried into Egypt.3 ✦2 Yahweh also has a controversy with Judah, and will punish Jacob according to his ways; according to his deeds he will repay him.45 ✦3 In the womb he took his brother by the heel, and in his manhood he contended with God6.7 ✦4 Indeed, he struggled with the angel, and prevailed; he wept, and made supplication to him. He found him at Bethel, and there he spoke with us —8 ✦5 even Yahweh, the God9 of Armies. Yahweh is his name of renown!1011 ✦6 Therefore turn to your God12. Keep kindness and justice, and wait continually for your God.1314 ✦7 A merchant has dishonest scales in his hand. He loves to defraud.15 ✦8 Ephraim said, “Surely I have become rich. I have found myself wealth. In all my wealth they won’t find in me any iniquity that is sin16.”17 ✦9 “But I am Yahweh your God18 from the land19 of Egypt. I will yet again make you dwell in tents, as in the days of the solemn feast.2021 10 I have also spoken to the prophets, and I have multiplied visions; and by the ministry of the prophets I have used parables.22 ✦11 If Gilead is wicked, surely they are worthless. In Gilgal they sacrifice bulls. Indeed, their altars are like heaps in the furrows of the field.23 12 Jacob fled into the country of Aram. Israel served to get a wife. For a wife he tended flocks and herds.24 13 By a prophet Yahweh brought Israel up out of Egypt, and by a prophet he was preserved.25 14 Ephraim has bitterly provoked anger. Therefore his blood26 will be left on him, and his Lord27 will repay his contempt.

Bel and the Dragon 1:1.

And king Astyages was gathered to his fathers, and Cyrus of Persia received his kingdom.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ΚΑΙ ὁ βασιλεὺς Ἀστυάγης προσετέθη πρὸς τοὺς πατέρας αὐτοῦ, καὶ παρέλαβεν Κῦρος ὁ Πέρσης τὴν βασιλείαν αὐτοῦ.

Douay-Rheims

And king Astyages was gathered to his fathers, and Cyrus the Persian received his kingdom.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et rex Astyages appositus est ad patres suos, et suscepit Cyrus Perses regnum ejus.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:2.

And Daniel conversed with the king, and was honoured above all his friends.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἦν Δανιὴλ συνβιωτὴς τοῦ βασιλέως καὶ ἔνδοξος ὑπὲρ πάντας τοὺς φίλους αὐτοῦ.

Douay-Rheims

And Daniel was the king’s guest, and was honoured above all his friends.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Erat autem Daniel conviva regis, et honoratus super omnes amicos ejus.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:3.

Now the Babylons had an idol, called Bel, and there were spent upon him every day twelve great measures of fine flour, and forty sheep, and six vessels of wine.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἦν εἴδωλον τοῖς Βαβυλωνίοις ᾧ ὄνομα Βήλ, καὶ ἐδαπανῶντο εἰς αὐτὸν ἑκάστης ἡμέρας σεμιδάλεως ἀρτάβαι δώδεκα καὶ πρόβατα τεσσεράκοντα καὶ οἴνου μετρηταὶ ἕξ.

Douay-Rheims

Now the Babylonians had an idol called Bel: and there were spent upon him every day twelve great measures of fine flour, and forty sheep, and sixty vessels of wine.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Erat quoque idolum apud Babylonios nomine Bel: et impendebantur in eo per dies singulos similæ artabæ duodecim, et oves quadraginta, vinique amphoræ sex.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:4.

And the king worshipped it and went daily to adore it: but Daniel worshipped his own God. And the king said unto him, Why dost not thou worship Bel?

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ὁ βασιλεὺς ἐσέβετο αὐτόν, καὶ ἐπορεύετο καθ᾽ ἑκάστην ἡμέραν προσκυνεῖν αὐτῷ, Δανιὴλ δὲ προσεκύνει τῷ θεῷ αὐτοῦ· καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ ὁ βασιλεύς Διὰ τί οὐ προσκυνεῖς τῷ Βήλ;

Douay-Rheims

The king also worshipped him, and went every day to adore him: but Daniel adored his God. And the king said to him: Why dost thou not adore Bel?

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Rex quoque colebat eum, et ibat per singulos dies adorare eum: porro Daniel adorabat Deum suum. Dixitque ei rex: Quare non adoras Bel?

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:5.

Who answered and said, Because I may not worship idols made with hands, but the living God, who hath created the heaven and the earth, and hath sovereignty over all flesh.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ὁ δὲ εἶπεν Ὅτι οὐ σέβομαι εἴδωλα χειροποίητα, ἀλλὰ τὸν ζῶντα θεόν, τὸν κτίσαντα τὸν οὐρανὸν καὶ τὴν γῆν καὶ ἔχοντα πάσης σαρκὸς κυρίαν.

Douay-Rheims

And he answered, and said to him: Because I do not worship idols made with hands, but the living God, that created heaven and earth, and hath power over all flesh.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Qui respondens ait ei: Quia non colo idola manufacta, sed viventem Deum, qui creavit cælum, et terram, et habet potestatem omnis carnis.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:6.

Then said the king unto him, Thinkest thou not that Bel is a living God? seest thou not how much he eateth and drinketh every day?

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ ὁ βασιλεύς Οὐ δοκεῖ σοι Βὴλ εἶναι ζῶν θεός; ἢ οὐχ ὁρᾷς ὅσα ἐσθίει καὶ πίνει καθ᾽ ἑκάστην ἡμέραν;

Douay-Rheims

And the king said to him: Doth not Bel seem to thee to be a living god? Seest thou not how much he eateth and drinketh every day?

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et dixit rex ad eum: Non videtur tibi esse Bel vivens deus? an non vides quanta comedat et bibat quotidie?

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:7.

Then Daniel smiled, and said, O king, be not deceived: for this is but clay within, and brass without, and did never eat or drink any thing.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν Δανιὴλ γελάσας Μὴ πλανῶ, βασιλεῦ· οὗτος γὰρ ἔσωθέν ἐστι πηλός, ἔξωθεν δὲ χαλκός, καὶ οὐ βέβρωκεν οὐδέποτε.

Douay-Rheims

Then Daniel smiled and said: O king, be not deceived: for this is but clay within, and brass without, neither hath he eaten at any time.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et ait Daniel arridens: Ne erres, rex: iste enim intrinsecus luteus est, et forinsecus æreus, neque comedit aliquando.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:8.

So the king was wroth, and called for his priests, and said unto them, If ye tell me not who this is that devoureth these expences, ye shall die.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ θυμωθεὶς ὁ βασιλεὺς ἐκάλεσεν τοὺς ἱερεῖς αὐτοῦ καὶ εἶπεν αὐτοῖς Ἐὰν μὴ εἴπητέ μοι τίς ὁ κατέσθων τὴν δαπάνην ταύτην, ἀποθανεῖσθε·

Douay-Rheims

And the king being angry called for his priests, and said to them: If you tell me not, who it is that eateth up these expenses, you shall die.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et iratus rex vocavit sacerdotes ejus, et ait eis: Nisi dixeritis mihi quis est qui comedat impensas has, moriemini.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:9.

But if ye can certify me that Bel devoureth them, then Daniel shall die: for he hath spoken blasphemy against Bel. And Daniel said unto the king, Let it be according to thy word.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ἐὰν δὲ δείξητε ὅτι Βὴλ κατεσθίει αὐτά, ἀποθανεῖται Δανιήλ, ὅτι ἐβλασφήμησεν εἰς τὸν Βήλ. καὶ εἶπεν Δανιὴλ τῷ βασιλεῖ Γινέσθω κατὰ τὸ ῥῆμά σου.

Douay-Rheims

But if you can shew that Bel eateth these things, Daniel shall die, because he hath blasphemed against Bel. And Daniel said to the king: Be it done according to thy word.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Si autem ostenderitis quoniam Bel comedat hæc, morietur Daniel, quia blasphemavit in Bel. Et dixit Daniel regi: Fiat juxta verbum tuum.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:10.

Now the priests of Bel were threescore and ten, beside their wives and children. And the king went with Daniel into the temple of Bel.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἦσαν ἱερεῖς τοῦ Βὴλ ἑβδομήκοντα ἐκτὸς γυναικῶν καὶ τέκνων· καὶ ἦλθεν ὁ βασιλεὺς μετὰ Δανιὴλ εἰς τὸν οἶκον τοῦ Βήλ.

Douay-Rheims

Now the priests of Bel were seventy, besides their wives, and little ones, and children. And the king went with Daniel into the temple of Bel.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Erant autem sacerdotes Bel septuaginta, exceptis uxoribus, et parvulis, et filiis. Et venit rex cum Daniele in templum Bel.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:11.

So Bel’s priests said, Lo, we go out: but thou, O king, set on the meat, and make ready the wine, and shut the door fast and seal it with thine own signet;

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπαν οἱ ἱερεῖς τοῦ Βήλ Ἰδοὺ ἡμεῖς ἀποτρέχομεν ἔξω, σὺ δέ, βασιλεῦ, παράθες τὰ βρώματα καὶ τὸν οἶνον κεράσας θές, καὶ ἀπόκλεισον τὴν θὺραν καὶ σφράγισον τῷ δακτυλίῳ σου.

Douay-Rheims

And the priests of Bel said: Behold we go out: and do thou, O king, set on the meats, and make ready the wine, and shut the door fast, and seal it with thy own ring:

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et dixerunt sacerdotes Bel: Ecce nos egredimur foras: et tu, rex, pone escas, et vinum misce, et claude ostium, et signa annulo tuo:

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:12.

And to morrow when thou comest in, if thou findest not that Bel hath eaten up all, we will suffer death: or else Daniel, that speaketh falsely against us.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἐλθὼν πρωὶ ἐὰν μὴ εὕρῃς πὰντα βεβρωμένα ὑπὸ τοῦ Βήλ, ἀποθανούμεθα, ἢ Δανιὴλ ὁ ψευδόμενος καθ᾽ ἡμῶν.

Douay-Rheims

And when thou comest in the morning, if thou findest not that Bel hath eaten up all, we will suffer death, or else Daniel that hath lied against us.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

et cum ingressus fueris mane, nisi inveneris omnia comesta a Bel, morte moriemur, vel Daniel qui mentitus est adversum nos.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:13.

And they little regarded it: for under the table they had made a privy entrance, whereby they entered in continually, and consumed those things.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

αὐτοὶ δὲ κατεφρόνουν, ὅτι πεποιήκεισαν ὑπὸ τὴν τράπεζαν κεκρυμμένην εἴσοδον, καὶ δι᾽ αὐτῆς εἰσεπορεύοντο διόλου καὶ ἀνήλουν αὐτά.

Douay-Rheims

And they little regarded it, because they had made under the table a secret entrance, and they always came in by it, and consumed those things.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Contemnebant autem, quia fecerant sub mensa absconditum introitum, et per illum ingrediebantur semper, et devorabant ea.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:14.

So when they were gone forth, the king set meats before Bel. Now Daniel had commanded his servants to bring ashes, and those they strewed throughout all the temple in the presence of the king alone: then went they out, and shut the door, and sealed it with the king’s signet, and so departed.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἐγένετο ὡς ἐξήλθοσαν ἐκεῖνοι, καὶ ὁ βασιλεὺς παρέθηκεν τὰ βρώματα τῷ Βήλ· καὶ ἐπέταξεν Δανιὴλ τοῖς παιδαρὶοις αὐτοῦ καὶ ἤνεγκαν τέφραν, καὶ κατέσησαν ὅλον τὸν ναὸν ἐνώπιον τοῦ βασιλέως μόνου· καὶ ἐξελθόντες ἔκλεισαν τὴν θύραν καὶ ἐσφραγίσαντο ἐν τῷ δακτυλίῳ τοῦ βασιλέως, καὶ ἀπῆλθον.

Douay-Rheims

So it came to pass after they were gone out, the king set the meats before Bel: and Daniel commanded his servants, and they brought ashes, and he sifted them all over the temple before the king: and going forth they shut the door, and having sealed it with the king’s ring, they departed.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Factum est igitur postquam egressi sunt illi, rex posuit cibos ante Bel: præcepit Daniel pueris suis, et attulerunt cinerem, et cribravit per totum templum coram rege: et egressi clauserunt ostium, et signantes annulo regis abierunt.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:15.

Now in the night came the priests with their wives and children, as they were wont to do, and did eat and drink up all.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

οἱ δὲ ἱερεῖς ἦλθον τὴν νύκτα κατὰ τὸ ἔθος αὐτῶν καὶ αἱ γυναῖκες καὶ τὰ τέκνα αὐτῶν, καὶ κατέφαγον πάντα καὶ ἐξέπιον.

Douay-Rheims

But the priests went in by night, according to their custom, with their wives and their children: and they ate and drank up all.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Sacerdotes autem ingressi sunt nocte juxta consuetudinem suam, et uxores et filii eorum, et comederunt omnia, et biberunt.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:16.

In the morning betime the king arose, and Daniel with him.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ὤρθρισεν ὁ βασιλεὺς πρωί, καὶ Δανιὴλ μετ᾽ αὐτοῦ.

Douay-Rheims

And the king arose early in the morning, and Daniel with him.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Surrexit autem rex primo diluculo, et Daniel cum eo.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:17.

And the king said, Daniel, are the seals whole? And he said, Yea, O king, they be whole.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν Σῷοι αἱ σφραγῖδες, Δανιήλ; ὁ δὲ εἶπεν Σῷοι, βασιλεῦ.

Douay-Rheims

And the king said: Are the seals whole, Daniel? And he answered: They are whole, O king.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et ait rex: Salvane sunt signacula, Daniel? Qui respondit: Salva, rex.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:18.

And as soon as he had opened the door, the king looked upon the table, and cried with a loud voice, Great art thou, O Bel, and with thee is no deceit at all.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἐγένετο ἅμα τῷ ἀνοῖξαι τὰς θύρας, ἐπιβλέψας ὁ βασιλεὺς ἐπὶ τὴν τράπεζαν ἐβόησεν φωνῇ μεγάλῃ Μέγας εἶ, Βήλ, καὶ οὐκ ἔστιν παρὰ σοὶ δόλος οὐδὲ εἷς.

Douay-Rheims

And as soon as he had opened the door, the king looked upon the table, and cried out with a loud voice: Great art thou, O Bel, and there is not any deceit with thee.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Statimque cum aperuisset ostium, intuitus rex mensam, exclamavit voce magna: Magnus es, Bel, et non est apud te dolus quisquam.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:19.

Then laughed Daniel, and held the king that he should not go in, and said, Behold now the pavement, and mark well whose footsteps are these.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἐγέλασεν Δανιήλ, καὶ ἐκράτησεν τὸν βασιλέα τοῦ μὴ εἰσελθεῖν αὐτὸν ἔσω, καὶ εἶπεν Ἴδε δὴ τὸ ἔδαφος καὶ γνῶθι τὶνος τὰ ἴχνη ταῦτα.

Douay-Rheims

And Daniel laughed: and he held the king that he should not go in: and he said: Behold the pavement, mark whose footsteps these are.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et risit Daniel, et tenuit regem ne ingrederetur intro: et dixit: Ecce pavimentum: animadverte cujus vestigia sint hæc.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:20.

And the king said, I see the footsteps of men, women, and children. And then the king was angry,

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν ὁ βασιλεύς Ὁρῶ τὰ ἴχνη ἀνδρῶν καὶ γυναικῶν καὶ παιδίων.

Douay-Rheims

And the king said: I see the footsteps of men, and women, and children. And the king was angry.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et dixit rex: Video vestigia virorum, et mulierum, et infantium. Et iratus est rex.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:21.

And took the priests with their wives and children, who shewed him the privy doors, where they came in, and consumed such things as were upon the table.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ὀργισθεὶς ὁ βασιλεὺς τὸτε συνέλαβεν τοὺς ἱερεῖς καὶ τὰς γυναῖκας καὶ τὰ τέκνα αὐτῶν, καὶ ἔδειξαν αὐτῷ τὰς κρυπτὰς θύρας δι᾽ ὧν εἰσεπορεύοντο καὶ ἐδαπάνων τὰ ἐπὶ τῇ τραπέζῃ.

Douay-Rheims

Then he took the priests, and their wives, and their children: and they shewed him the private doors by which they came in, and consumed the things that were on the table.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Tunc apprehendit sacerdotes, et uxores, et filios eorum: et ostenderunt ei abscondita ostiola, per quæ ingrediebantur, et consumebant quæ erant super mensam.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:22.

Therefore the king slew them, and delivered Bel into Daniel’s power, who destroyed him and his temple.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἀπέκτεινεν αὐτοὺς ὁ βασιλεὺς, καὶ ἔδωκεν τὸν Βὴλ ἔκδοτον τῷ Δανιήλ· καὶ κατέστρεψεν αὐτὸν καὶ τὸ ἱερὸν αὐτοῦ.

Douay-Rheims

The king therefore put them to death, and delivered Bel into the power of Daniel: who destroyed him, and his temple.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Occidit ergo illos rex, et tradidit Bel in potestatem Danielis: qui subvertit eum, et templum ejus.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:23.

And in that same place there was a great dragon, which they of Babylon worshipped.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

Καὶ ἦν δράκων μέγας, καὶ ἐσέβοντο αὐτὸν οἱ Βαβυλώνιοι.

Douay-Rheims

And there was a great dragon in that place, and the Babylonians worshipped him.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et erat draco magnus in loco illo, et colebant eum Babylonii.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:24.

And the king said unto Daniel, Wilt thou also say that this is of brass? lo, he liveth, he eateth and drinketh; thou canst not say that he is no living god: therefore worship him.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν ὁ βασιλεὺς τῷ Δανιήλ Οὐ δύνασαι εἰπεῖν ὅτι οὐκ ἔστιν οὗτος θεὸς ζῶν· καὶ προσκύνησον αὐτῷ.

Douay-Rheims

And the king said to Daniel: Behold thou canst not say now, that this is not a living god: adore him therefore.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et dixit rex Danieli: Ecce nunc non potes dicere quia iste non sit deus vivens: adora ergo eum.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:25.

Then said Daniel unto the king, I will worship the Lord my God: for he is the living God.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν Δανιήλ Κυρίῳ τῷ θεῷ μου προσκυνήσω, ὅτι οὗτός ἐστιν θεὸς ζῶν.

Douay-Rheims

And Daniel said: I adore the Lord my God: for he is the living God: but that is no living god.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Dixitque Daniel: Dominum Deum meum adoro, quia ipse est Deus vivens: iste autem non est deus vivens.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:26.

But give me leave, O king, and I shall slay this dragon without sword or staff. The king said, I give thee leave.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

σὺ δέ, βασιλεῦ, δός μοι ἐξουσίαν, καὶ ἀποκτενῶ τὸν δράκοντα ἄνευ μαχαίρας καὶ ῥάβδου. καὶ εἶπεν ὁ βασιλεύς Δίδωμί σοι.

Douay-Rheims

But give me leave, O king, and I will kill this dragon without sword or club. And the king said: I give thee leave.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Tu autem, rex, da mihi potestatem, et interficiam draconem absque gladio et fuste. Et ait rex: Do tibi.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:27.

Then Daniel took pitch, and fat, and hair, and did seethe them together, and made lumps thereof: this he put in the dragon’s mouth, and so the dragon burst in sunder: and Daniel said, Lo, these are the gods ye worship.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἔλαβεν Δανιὴλ πίσσαν καὶ στῆρ καὶ τρίχας, καὶ ἥψησεν ἐπὶ τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ ἐποίησεν μάζας, καὶ ἔδωκεν εἰς τὸ στόμα τοῦ δράκοντος· καὶ φαγὼν διερράγη ὁ δράκων. καὶ εἶπεν Ἴδετε τὰ σεβάσματα ὑμῶν.

Douay-Rheims

Then Daniel took pitch, and fat, and hair, and boiled them together: and he made lumps, and put them into the dragon’s mouth, and the dragon burst asunder. And he said: Behold him whom you worshipped.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Tulit ergo Daniel picem, et adipem, et pilos, et coxit pariter: fecitque massas, et dedit in os draconis, et diruptus est draco. Et dixit: Ecce quem colebatis.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:28.

When they of Babylon heard that, they took great indignation, and conspired against the king, saying, The king is become a Jew, and he hath destroyed Bel, he hath slain the dragon, and put the priests to death.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἐγένετο ὡς ἤκουσαν οἱ Βαβυλώνιοι, ἠγανάκτησαν καὶ συνεστράφησαν ἐπὶ τὸν βασιλέα καὶ εἶπαν Ἰουδαῖος γέγονεν ὁ βασιλεύς· τὸν Βὴλ κατέσπασεν καὶ τὸν δράκοντα ἀπέκτεινεν, καὶ τοὺς ἱερεῖς κατέσφαξεν.

Douay-Rheims

And when the Babylonians had heard this, they took great indignation: and being gathered together against the king, they said: The king is become a Jew. He hath destroyed Bel, he hath killed the dragon, and he hath put the priests to death.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Quod cum audissent Babylonii, indignati sunt vehementer: et congregati adversum regem, dixerunt: Judæus factus est rex: Bel destruxit, draconem interfecit, et sacerdotes occidit.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:29.

So they came to the king, and said, Deliver us Daniel, or else we will destroy thee and thine house.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπαν ἐλθόντες πρὸς τὸν βασιλέα Παράδος ἡμῖν τὸν Δανιήλ· εἰ δὲ μή, ἀποκτενοῦμέν σε καὶ τὸν οἶκόν σου.

Douay-Rheims

And they came to the king, and said: Deliver us Daniel, or else we will destroy thee and thy house.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et dixerunt cum venissent ad regem: Trade nobis Danielem, alioquin interficiemus te, et domum tuam.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:30.

Now when the king saw that they pressed him sore, being constrained, he delivered Daniel unto them:

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἴδεν ὁ βασιλεὺς ὅτι ἐπείγουσιν αὐτὸν σφόδρα, καὶ ἀναγκασθεὶς παρέδωκεν αὐτοῖς τὸν Δανιήλ.

Douay-Rheims

And the king saw that they pressed upon him violently: and being constrained by necessity he delivered Daniel to them.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Vidit ergo rex quod irruerent in eum vehementer: et necessitate compulsus, tradidit eis Danielem.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:31.

Who cast him into the lions’ den: where he was six days.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

οἱ δὲ ἔβαλον αὐτὸν εἰς τὸν λάκκον τῶν λεόντων, καὶ ἦν ἐκεῖ ἡμέρας ἕξ.

Douay-Rheims

And they cast him into the den of lions, and he was there six days.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Qui miserunt eum in lacum leonum, et erat ibi diebus sex.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:32.

And in the den there were seven lions, and they had given them every day two carcases, and two sheep: which then were not given to them, to the intent they might devour Daniel.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ἦσαν δὲ ἐν τῷ λάκκῳ ἑπτὰ λέοντες, καὶ ἐδίδετο αὐτοῖς τὴν ἡμέραν δύο σώματα καὶ δύο πρόβατα· τότε δὲ οὐκ ἐδόθη αὐτοῖς, ἵνα καταφάγωσιν τὸν Δανιήλ.

Douay-Rheims

And in the den there were seven lions, and they had given to them two carcasses every day, and two sheep: but then they were not given unto them, that they might devour Daniel.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Porro in lacu erant leones septem, et dabantur eis duo corpora quotidie, et duæ oves: et tunc non data sunt eis, ut devorarent Danielem.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:33.

Now there was in Jewry a prophet, called Habbacuc, who had made pottage, and had broken bread in a bowl, and was going into the field, for to bring it to the reapers.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἦν Ἁμβακοὺμ ὁ προφήτης ἐν τῇ Ἰουδαίᾳ, καὶ αὐτὸς ἥψησεν ἕψεμα καὶ ἐνέθρυψεν ἄρτους εἰς σκάφην, καὶ ἐπορεύετο εἰς τὸ πεδίον ἀπενέγκαι τοῖς θερισταῖς.

Douay-Rheims

Now there was in Judea a prophet called Habacuc, and he had boiled pottage, and had broken bread in a bowl: and was going into the field, to carry it to the reapers.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Erat autem Habacuc propheta in Judæa, et ipse coxerat pulmentum, et intriverat panes in alveolo: et ibat in campum ut ferret messoribus.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:34.

But the angel of the Lord said unto Habbacuc, Go, carry the dinner that thou hast into Babylon unto Daniel, who is in the lions’ den.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν ἄγγελος Κυρίου τῷ Ἁμβακούμ Ἀπένεγκε τὸ ἄριστον ὃ ἔχεις εἰς Βαβυλῶνα τῷ Δανιὴλ εἰς λάκκον τῶν λεόντων.

Douay-Rheims

And the angel of the Lord said to Habacuc: Carry the dinner which thou hast into Babylon to Daniel, who is in the lions’ den.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Dixitque angelus Domini ad Habacuc: Fer prandium quod habes in Babylonem Danieli, qui est in lacu leonum.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:35.

And Habbacuc said, Lord, I never saw Babylon; neither do I know where the den is.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν Ἁμβακούμ κύριε, Βαβυλῶνα οὐχ ἑώρακα, καὶ τὸν λάκκον οὐ γινώσκω

Douay-Rheims

And Habacuc said: Lord, I never saw Babylon, nor do I know the den.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et dixit Habacuc: Domine, Babylonem non vidi, et lacum nescio.

↩

Bel and the Dragon 1:36.

Then the angel of the Lord took him by the crown, and bare him by the hair of his head, and through the vehemency of his spirit set him in Babylon over the den.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἐπελάβετο ὁ ἄγγελος Κυρίου τῆς κορυφῆς αὐτοῦ, καὶ βαστάσας τῆς κόμης τῆς κεφαλῆς αὐτοῦ ἔθηκεν αὐτὸν εἰς Βαβυλῶνα ἐπάνω τοῦ λάκκου, ἐν τῷ ῥοίζῳ τοῦ πνεύματος αὐτοῦ.

Douay-Rheims

And the angel of the Lord took him by the top of his head, and carried him by the hair of his head, and set him in Babylon over the den in the force of his spirit.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et apprehendit eum angelus Domini in vertice ejus, et portavit eum capillo capitis sui, posuitque eum in Babylone supra lacum in impetu spiritus sui.

Bel and the Dragon 1:37.

And Habbacuc cried, saying, O Daniel, Daniel, take the dinner which God hath sent thee.

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

Douay-Rheims

And Habacuc cried, saying: O Daniel, thou servant of God, take the dinner that God hath sent thee.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et clamavit Habacuc, dicens: Daniel serve Dei, tolle prandium quod misit tibi Deus.

Douay-Rheims

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

The Book of Hosea 1:1.

The word of the LORD that came unto Hosea, the son of Beeri, in the days of Uzziah, Jotham, Ahaz, [and] Hezekiah, kings of Judah, and in the days of Jeroboam the son of Joash, king of Israel.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

דְּבַר־יְהוָ֣ה ׀ אֲשֶׁ֣ר הָיָ֗ה אֶל־הוֹשֵׁ֨עַ֙ בֶּן־בְּאֵרִ֔י בִּימֵ֨י עֻזִּיָּ֥ה יוֹתָ֛ם אָחָ֥ז יְחִזְקִיָּ֖ה מַלְכֵ֣י יְהוּדָ֑ה וּבִימֵ֛י יָרָבְעָ֥ם בֶּן־יוֹאָ֖שׁ מֶ֥לֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵֽל׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ΛΟΓΟΣ Κυρίου ὃς ἐγενήθη πρὸς Ὡσῆε τὸν τοῦ Βεηρεὶ ἐν ἡμέραις Ὀζείου καὶ Ἰωαθὰμ καὶ Ἀχὰς καὶ Ἑζεκίου βασιλέων Ἰούδα καὶ ἐν ἡμέραις Ἰεροβοὰμ υἱοῦ Ἰωὰς βασιλέως Ἰσραήλ.

Douay-Rheims

The word of the Lord, that came to Osee the son of Beeri, in the days of Ozias, Joathan, Achaz, and Ezechias kings of Juda, and in the days of Jeroboam the son of Joas king of Israel.

JPS (1917)

THE WORD of the LORD that came unto Hosea the son of Beeri, in the days of Uzziah, Jotham, Ahaz, and Hezekiah, kings of Judah, and in the days of Jeroboam the son of Joash, king of Israel.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Verbum Domini, quod factum est ad Osee, filium Beeri, in diebus Oziæ, Joathan, Achaz, Ezechiæ, regum Juda; et in diebus Jeroboam, filii Joas, regis Israël.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

قَوْلُ ٱلرَّبِّ ٱلَّذِي صَارَ إِلَى هُوشَعَ بْنِ بِئِيرِي، فِي أَيَّامِ عُزِّيَّا وَيُوثَامَ وَآحَازَ وَحَزَقِيَّا مُلُوكِ يَهُوذَا، وَفِي أَيَّامِ يَرُبْعَامَ بْنِ يُوآشَ مَلِكِ إِسْرَائِيلَ.

The Book of Hosea 1:2.

The beginning of the word of the LORD by Hosea. And the LORD said to Hosea, Go, take unto thee a wife of whoredoms and children of whoredoms: for the land hath committed great whoredom, [departing] from the LORD.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

תְּחִלַּ֥ת דִּבֶּר־יְהוָ֖ה בְּהוֹשֵׁ֑עַ וַיֹּ֨אמֶר יְהוָ֜ה אֶל־הוֹשֵׁ֗עַ לֵ֣ךְ קַח־לְךָ֞ אֵ֤שֶׁת זְנוּנִים֙ וְיַלְדֵ֣י זְנוּנִ֔ים כִּֽי־זָנֹ֤ה תִזְנֶה֙ הָאָ֔רֶץ מֵֽאַחֲרֵ֖י יְהוָֽה׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

Ἀρχὴ λόγου Κυρίου ἐν Ὡσῆε. καὶ εἶπεν Κύριος πρὸς Ὡσηέ Βάδιζε λάβε σεαυτῷ γυναῖκα πορνείας καὶ τέκνα πορνείας, διότι ἐκπορνεύουσα ἐκπορνεύσει ἡ γῆ ἀπὸ ὄπισθεν τοῦ κυρίου.

Douay-Rheims

The beginning of the Lord’s speaking by Osse: and the Lord said to Osee: Go, take thee a wife of fornications, and have of her children of fornications: for the land by fornication shall depart from the Lord.

JPS (1917)

When the LORD spoke at first with Hosea, the LORD said unto Hosea: 'Go, take unto thee a wife of harlotry and children of harlotry; for the land doth commit great harlotry, departing from the LORD.'

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Principium loquendi Domino in Osee. Et dixit Dominus ad Osee: Vade, sume tibi uxorem fornicationum, et fac tibi filios fornicationum, quia fornicans fornicabitur terra a Domino.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

أَوَّلَ مَا كَلَّمَ ٱلرَّبُّ هُوشَعَ، قَالَ ٱلرَّبُّ لِهُوشَعَ: «ٱذْهَبْ خُذْ لِنَفْسِكَ ٱمْرَأَةَ زِنًى وَأَوْلَادَ زِنًى، لِأَنَّ ٱلْأَرْضَ قَدْ زَنَتْ زِنًى تَارِكَةً ٱلرَّبَّ».

The Book of Hosea 1:4.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וַיֹּ֤אמֶר יְהוָה֙ אֵלָ֔יו קְרָ֥א שְׁמ֖וֹ יִזְרְעֶ֑אל כִּי־ע֣וֹד מְעַ֗ט וּפָ֨קַדְתִּ֜י אֶת־דְּמֵ֤י יִזְרְעֶאל֙ עַל־בֵּ֣ית יֵה֔וּא וְהִ֨שְׁבַּתִּ֔י מַמְלְכ֖וּת בֵּ֥ית יִשְׂרָאֵֽל׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ εἶπεν Κύριος πρὸς αὐτόν Κάλεσον τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ Ἰεζραέλ, διότι ἔτι μικρὸν καὶ ἐκδικήσω τὸ αἷμα τοῦ Ἰεζραὲλ ἐπὶ τὸν οἶκον Ἰούδα, καὶ καταπαύσω βασιλείαν οἴκου Ἰσραήλ·

Douay-Rheims

And the Lord said to him: Call his name Jezrahel: for yet a little while, and I will visit the blood of Jezrahel upon the house of Jehu, and I will cause to cease the kingdom of the house of Israel.

JPS (1917)

And the LORD said unto him: 'Call his name Jezreel; for yet a little while, and I will visit the blood of Jezreel upon the house of Jehu, and will cause to cease the kingdom of the house of Israel.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et dixit Dominus ad eum: Voca nomen ejus Jezrahel, quoniam adhuc modicum, et visitabo sanguinem Jezrahel super domum Jehu, et quiescere faciam regnum domus Israël.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

فَقَالَ لَهُ ٱلرَّبُّ: «ٱدْعُ ٱسْمَهُ يَزْرَعِيلَ، لِأَنَّنِي بَعْدَ قَلِيلٍ أُعَاقِبُ بَيْتَ يَاهُو عَلَى دَمِ يَزْرَعِيلَ، وَأُبِيدُ مَمْلَكَةَ بَيْتِ إِسْرَائِيلَ.

The Book of Hosea 1:6.

¶ And she conceived again, and bare a daughter. And [God] said unto him, Call her name Lo-ruhamah: for I will no more have mercy upon the house of Israel; but I will utterly take them away.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וַתַּ֤הַר עוֹד֙ וַתֵּ֣לֶד בַּ֔ת וַיֹּ֣אמֶר ל֔וֹ קְרָ֥א שְׁמָ֖הּ לֹ֣א רֻחָ֑מָה כִּי֩ לֹ֨א אוֹסִ֜יף ע֗וֹד אֲרַחֵם֙ אֶת־בֵּ֣ית יִשְׂרָאֵ֔ל כִּֽי־נָשֹׂ֥א אֶשָּׂ֖א לָהֶֽם׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ συνέλαβεν ἔτι καὶ ἔτεκεν θυγατέρα. καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ Κάλεσον τὸ ὄνομα αὐτῆς Οὐκ ἠλεημένη, διότι οὐ μὴ προσθήσω ἔτι ἐλεῆσαι τὸν οἶκον Ἰσραήλ, ἀλλ᾽ ἢ ἀντιτασσόμενος ἀντιτάξομαι αὐτοῖς.

Douay-Rheims

And she conceived again, and bore a daughter, and he said to him: Call her name, Without mercy: for I will not add any more to have mercy on the house of Israel, but I will utterly forget them.

JPS (1917)

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et concepit adhuc, et peperit filiam. Et dixit ei: Voca nomen ejus, Absque misericordia, quia non addam ultra misereri domui Israël, sed oblivione obliviscar eorum.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

ثُمَّ حَبِلَتْ أَيْضًا وَوَلَدَتْ بِنْتًا، فَقَالَ لَهُ: «ٱدْعُ ٱسْمَهَا لُورُحَامَةَ، لِأَنِّي لَا أَعُودُ أَرْحَمُ بَيْتَ إِسْرَائِيلَ أَيْضًا، بَلْ أَنْزِعُهُمْ نَزْعًا.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

The Book of Hosea 1:10.

¶ Yet the number of the children of Israel shall be as the sand of the sea, which cannot be measured nor numbered; and it shall come to pass, [that] in the place where it was said unto them, Ye [are] not my people, [there] it shall be said unto them, [Ye are] the sons of the living God.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וְֽ֠הָיָה מִסְפַּ֤ר בְּנֵֽי־יִשְׂרָאֵל֙ כְּח֣וֹל הַיָּ֔ם אֲשֶׁ֥ר לֹֽא־יִמַּ֖ד וְלֹ֣א יִסָּפֵ֑ר וְֽ֠הָיָה בִּמְק֞וֹם אֲשֶׁר־יֵאָמֵ֤ר לָהֶם֙ לֹֽא־עַמִּ֣י אַתֶּ֔ם יֵאָמֵ֥ר לָהֶ֖ם בְּנֵ֥י אֵֽל־חָֽי׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

Καὶ ἦν ὁ ἀριθμὸς τῶν υἱῶν Ἰσραὴλ ὡς ἡ ἄμμος τῆς θαλάσσης, ἣ οὐκ ἐκμετρηθήσεται οὐδὲ ἐξαριθμηθήσεται· καὶ ἔσται ἐν τῷ τόπῳ οὗ ἐρρέθη αὐτοῖς Οὐ λαός μου ὑμεῖς, κληθήσονται καὶ αὐτοὶ υἱοὶ θεοῦ ζῶντος.

Douay-Rheims

And the number of the children of Israel shall be as the sand of the sea, that is without measure, and shall not be numbered. And it shall be in the place where it shall be said to them: You are not my people: it shall be said to them: Ye are the sons of the living God.

JPS (1917)

(2-1) Yet the number of the children of Israel shall be as the sand of the sea, which cannot be measured nor numbered; and it shall come to pass that, instead of that which was said unto them: 'Ye are not My people', it shall be said unto them: 'Ye are the children of the living God.'

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et erit numerus filiorum Israël quasi arena maris, quæ sine mensura est, et non numerabitur. Et erit in loco ubi dicetur eis: Non populus meus vos: dicetur eis: Filii Dei viventis.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

لَكِنْ يَكُونُ عَدَدُ بَنِي إِسْرَائِيلَ كَرَمْلِ ٱلْبَحْرِ ٱلَّذِي لَا يُكَالُ وَلَا يُعَدُّ، وَيَكُونُ عِوَضًا عَنْ أَنْ يُقَالَ لَهُمْ: لَسْتُمْ شَعْبِي، يُقَالُ لَهُمْ: أَبْنَاءُ ٱللهِ ٱلْحَيِّ.

The Book of Hosea 1:11.

Douay-Rheims

Plead with your mother, plead: for she [is] not my wife, neither [am] I her husband: let her therefore put away her whoredoms out of her sight, and her adulteries from between her breasts;

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

רִ֤יבוּ בְאִמְּכֶם֙ רִ֔יבוּ כִּֽי־הִיא֙ לֹ֣א אִשְׁתִּ֔י וְאָנֹכִ֖י לֹ֣א אִישָׁ֑הּ וְתָסֵ֤ר זְנוּנֶ֨יהָ֙ מִפָּנֶ֔יה וְנַאֲפוּפֶ֖יהָ מִבֵּ֥ין שָׁדֶֽיהָ׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

Κρίθητε πρὸς τὴν μητέρα ὑμῶν, κρίθητε, ὅτι αὕτη οὐ γυνή μου καὶ ἐγὼ οὐκ ἀνὴρ αὐτῆς. καὶ ἐξαρῶ τὴν πορνίαν αὐτῆς ἐκ προσώπου μου καὶ τὴν μοιχείαν αὐτῆς ἐκ μέσου μαστῶν αὐτῆς,

Douay-Rheims

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Arabic (Van Dyck)

ne forte expoliem eam nudam, et statuam eam secundum diem nativitatis suæ, et ponam eam quasi solitudinem, et statuam eam velut terram inviam, et interficiam eam siti.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

The Book of Hosea 2:5.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

כִּ֤י זָֽנְתָה֙ אִמָּ֔ם הֹבִ֖ישָׁה הֽוֹרָתָ֑ם כִּ֣י אָמְרָ֗ה אֵלְכָ֞ה אַחֲרֵ֤י מְאַהֲבַי֙ נֹתְנֵ֤י לַחְמִי֙ וּמֵימַ֔י צַמְרִ֣י וּפִשְׁתִּ֔י שַׁמְנִ֖י וְשִׁקּוּיָֽי׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

Douay-Rheims

Arabic (Van Dyck)

«لِأَنَّ أُمَّهُمْ قَدْ زَنَتِ. ٱلَّتِي حَبِلَتْ بِهِمْ صَنَعَتْ خِزْيًا. لِأَنَّهَا قَالَتْ: أَذْهَبُ وَرَاءَ مُحِبِّيَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يُعْطُونَ خُبْزِي وَمَائِي، صُوفِي وَكَتَّانِي، زَيْتِي وَأَشْرِبَتِي.

The Book of Hosea 2:7.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

فَتَتْبَعُ مُحِبِّيهَا وَلَا تُدْرِكُهُمْ، وَتُفَتِّشُ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا تَجِدُهُمْ. فَتَقُولُ: أَذْهَبُ وَأَرْجِعُ إِلَى رَجُلِي ٱلْأَوَّلِ، لِأَنَّهُ حِينَئِذٍ كَانَ خَيْرٌ لِي مِنَ ٱلْآنَ.

Douay-Rheims

The Book of Hosea 2:9.

The Book of Hosea 2:10.

And now will I discover her lewdness in the sight of her lovers, and none shall deliver her out of mine hand.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וְעַתָּ֛ה אֲגַלֶּ֥ה אֶת־נַבְלֻתָ֖הּ לְעֵינֵ֣י מְאַהֲבֶ֑יהָ וְאִ֖ישׁ לֹֽא־יַצִּילֶ֥נָּה מִיָּדִֽי׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ νῦν ἀποκαλύψω τὴν ἀκαθαρσίαν αὐτῆς ἐνώπιον τῶν ἐραστῶν αὐτῆς, καὶ οὐδεὶς οὐ μὴ ἐξέληται αὐτὴν ἐκ χειρός μου·

Douay-Rheims

And now I will lay open her folly in the eyes of her lovers: and no man shall deliver her out of my hand:

JPS (1917)

(2-12) And now will I uncover her shame in the sight of her lovers, and none shall deliver her out of My hand.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et nunc revelabo stultitiam ejus in oculis amatorum ejus; et vir non eruet eam de manu mea;

Arabic (Van Dyck)

The Book of Hosea 2:12.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וַהֲשִׁמֹּתִ֗י גַּפְנָהּ֙ וּתְאֵ֣נָתָ֔הּ אֲשֶׁ֣ר אָמְרָ֗ה אֶתְנָ֥ה הֵ֨מָּה֙ לִ֔י אֲשֶׁ֥ר נָֽתְנוּ־לִ֖י מְאַֽהֲבָ֑י וְשַׂמְתִּ֣ים לְיַ֔עַר וַאֲכָלָ֖תַם חַיַּ֥ת הַשָּׂדֶֽה׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἀφανιῶ ἄμπελον αὐτῆς καὶ τὰς συκᾶς αὐτῆς, ὅσα εἶπεν Μισθώματά μου ταῦτά ἐστιν ἃ ἔδωκάν μοι οἱ ἐρασταί μου, καὶ θήσομαι αὐτὰ εἰς μαρτύριον, καὶ καταφάγεται αὐτὰ τὰ θηρία τοῦ ἀγροῦ καὶ τὰ πετεινὰ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ καὶ τὰ ἑρπετὰ τῆς γῆς·

Douay-Rheims

JPS (1917)

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Arabic (Van Dyck)

وَأُخَرِّبُ كَرْمَهَا وَتِينَهَا ٱللَّذَيْنِ قَالَتْ: هُمَا أُجْرَتِي ٱلَّتِي أَعْطَانِيهَا مُحِبِّيَّ، وَأَجْعَلُهُمَا وَعْرًا فَيَأْكُلُهُمَا حَيَوَانُ ٱلْبَرِّيَّةِ.

Douay-Rheims

Arabic (Van Dyck)

καὶ ἡ γῆ ἐπακούσεται τὸν σῖτον καὶ τὸν οἶνον καὶ τὸ ἔλαιον, καὶ αὐτὰ ἐπακούσεται τῷ Ἰεζραέλ.

Douay-Rheims

And the earth shall hear the core, and the wine, and the oil, and these shall hear Jezrahel.

JPS (1917)

(2-24) And the earth shall respond to the corn, and the wine, and the oil; and they shall respond to Jezreel.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et terra exaudiet triticum, et vinum, et oleum, et hæc exaudient Jezrahel.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

وَٱلْأَرْضُ تَسْتَجِيبُ ٱلْقَمْحَ وَٱلْمِسْطَارَ وَٱلزَّيْتَ، وَهِيَ تَسْتَجِيبُ يَزْرَعِيلَ.

The Book of Hosea 2:23.

And I will sow her unto me in the earth; and I will have mercy upon her that had not obtained mercy; and I will say to [them which were] not my people, Thou [art] my people; and they shall say, [Thou art] my God.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וּזְרַעְתִּ֤יהָ לִּי֙ בָּאָ֔רֶץ וְרִֽחַמְתִּ֖י אֶת־לֹ֣א רֻחָ֑מָה וְאָמַרְתִּ֤י לְלֹֽא־עַמִּי֙ עַמִּי־אַ֔תָּה וְה֖וּא יֹאמַ֥ר אֱלֹהָֽי׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ σπερῶ αὐτὴν ἐμαυτῷ ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς, καὶ ἀγαπήσω τὴν Οὐκ ἠγαπημένην, καὶ ἐρῶ τῷ Οὐ λαῷ μου Λαός μου εἶ σύ, καὶ αὐτὸς ἐρεῖ Κύριος ὁ θεός μου εἶ σύ.

Douay-Rheims

And I will sow her unto me in the earth, and I will have mercy on her that was without mercy. And I will say to that which was not my people: Thou art my people: and they shall say: Thou art my God.

JPS (1917)

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et seminabo eam mihi in terra, et miserebor ejus quæ fuit Absque misericordia. Et dicam Non populo meo: Populus meus es tu; et ipse dicet: Deus meus es tu.]

Arabic (Van Dyck)

وَأَزْرَعُهَا لِنَفْسِي فِي ٱلْأَرْضِ، وَأَرْحَمُ لُورُحَامَةَ، وَأَقُولُ لِلُوعَمِّي: أَنْتَ شَعْبِي، وَهُوَ يَقُولُ: أَنْتَ إِلَهِي».

The Book of Hosea 3:1.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

Douay-Rheims

Arabic (Van Dyck)

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Arabic (Van Dyck)

The Book of Hosea 4:6.

¶ My people are destroyed for lack of knowledge: because thou hast rejected knowledge, I will also reject thee, that thou shalt be no priest to me: seeing thou hast forgotten the law of thy God, I will also forget thy children.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

נִדְמ֥וּ עַמִּ֖י מִבְּלִ֣י הַדָּ֑עַת כִּֽי־אַתָּ֞ה הַדַּ֣עַת מָאַ֗סְתָּ וְאֶמְאָֽסְאךָ֙ מִכַּהֵ֣ן לִ֔י וַתִּשְׁכַּח֙ תּוֹרַ֣ת אֱלֹהֶ֔יךָ אֶשְׁכַּ֥ח בָּנֶ֖יךָ גַּם־אָֽנִי׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ὡμοιώθη ὁ λαός μου ὡς οὐκ ἔχων γνῶσιν· ὅτι σὺ ἐπίγνωσιν ἀπώσω, κἀγὼ ἀπώσομαι σὲ τοῦ ἱερατεύειν μοι· καὶ ἐπελάθου νόμον θεοῦ σου, κἀγὼ ἐπιλήσομαι τέκνων σου.

Douay-Rheims

My people have been silent, because they had no knowledge: because thou hast rejected knowledge, I will reject thee, that thou shalt not do the office of priesthood to me: and thou hast forgotten the law of thy God, I also will forget thy children.

JPS (1917)

My people are destroyed for lack of knowledge; because thou hast rejected knowledge, I will also reject thee, that thou shalt be no priest to Me; seeing thou hast forgotten the law of thy God, I also will forget thy children.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Conticuit populus meus, eo quod non habuerit scientiam: quia tu scientiam repulisti, repellam te, ne sacerdotio fungaris mihi; et oblita es legis Dei tui, obliviscar filiorum tuorum et ego.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

قَدْ هَلَكَ شَعْبِي مِنْ عَدَمِ ٱلْمَعْرِفَةِ. لِأَنَّكَ أَنْتَ رَفَضْتَ ٱلْمَعْرِفَةَ أَرْفُضُكَ أَنَا حَتَّى لَا تَكْهَنَ لِي. وَلِأَنَّكَ نَسِيتَ شَرِيعَةَ إِلَهِكَ أَنْسَى أَنَا أَيْضًا بَنِيكَ.

↩

The Book of Hosea 4:7.

As they were increased, so they sinned against me: [therefore] will I change their glory into shame.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

כְּרֻבָּ֖ם כֵּ֣ן חָֽטְאוּ־לִ֑י כְּבוֹדָ֖ם בְּקָל֥וֹן אָמִֽיר׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

κατὰ τὸ πλῆθος αὐτῶν οὕτως ἥμαρτόν μοι· τὴν δόξαν αὐτῶν εἰς ἀτιμίαν θήσομαι,

Douay-Rheims

According to the multitude of them so have they sinned against me: I will change their glory into shame.

JPS (1917)

The more they were increased, the more they sinned against Me; I will change their glory into shame.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Secundum multitudinem eorum sic peccaverunt mihi: gloriam eorum in ignominiam commutabo.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

عَلَى حَسْبَمَا كَثُرُوا، هَكَذَا أَخْطَأُوا إِلَيَّ، فَأُبْدِلُ كَرَامَتَهُمْ بِهَوَانٍ.

The Book of Hosea 4:8.

They eat up the sin of my people, and they set their heart on their iniquity.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

חַטַּ֥את עַמִּ֖י יֹאכֵ֑לוּ וְאֶל־עֲוֺנָ֖ם יִשְׂא֥וּ נַפְשֽׁוֹ׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ἁμαρτίας λαοῦ μου φάγονται, καὶ ἐν ταῖς ἀδικίαις αὐτῶν λήμψονται τὰς ψυχὰς αὐτῶν.

Douay-Rheims

They shall eat the sins of my people, and shall lift up their souls to their iniquity.

JPS (1917)

They feed on the sin of My people, and set their heart on their iniquity.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Peccata populi mei comedent, et ad iniquitatem eorum sublevabunt animas eorum.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

يَأْكُلُونَ خَطِيَّةَ شَعْبِي وَإِلَى إِثْمِهِمْ يَحْمِلُونَ نُفُوسَهُمْ.

The Book of Hosea 4:9.

And there shall be, like people, like priest: and I will punish them for their ways, and reward them their doings.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וְהָיָ֥ה כָעָ֖ם כַּכֹּהֵ֑ן וּפָקַדְתִּ֤י עָלָיו֙ דְּרָכָ֔יו וּמַעֲלָלָ֖יו אָשִׁ֥יב לֽוֹ׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ ἔσται καθὼς ὁ λαὸς οὕτως καὶ ὁ ἱερεύς, καὶ ἐκδικήσω ἐπ᾽ αὐτὸν τὰς ὁδοὺς αὐτοῦ, καὶ τὰ διαβούλια αὐτοῦ ἀνταποδώσω αὐτῷ.

Douay-Rheims

And there shall be like people like priest: and I will visit their ways upon them, and I will repay them their devices.

JPS (1917)

And it is like people, like priest; and I will punish him for his ways, and will recompense him his doings.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et erit sicut populus, sic sacerdos; et visitabo super eum vias ejus, et cogitationes ejus reddam ei.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

فَيَكُونُ كَمَا ٱلشَّعْبُ هَكَذَا ٱلْكَاهِنُ. وَأُعَاقِبُهُمْ عَلَى طُرُقِهِمْ وَأَرُدُّ أَعْمَالَهُمْ عَلَيْهِمْ.

The Book of Hosea 4:10.

For they shall eat, and not have enough: they shall commit whoredom, and shall not increase: because they have left off to take heed to the LORD.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

וְאָֽכְלוּ֙ וְלֹ֣א יִשְׂבָּ֔עוּ הִזְנ֖וּ וְלֹ֣א יִפְרֹ֑צוּ כִּֽי־אֶת־יְהוָ֥ה עָזְב֖וּ לִשְׁמֹֽר׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ φάγονται καὶ οὐ μὴ ἐμπλησθῶσιν, ἐπόρνευσαν καὶ οὐ μὴ κατευθύνωσιν, διότι τὸν κύριον ἐγκατέλιπον τοῦ φυλάξαι

Douay-Rheims

And they shall eat and shall not be filled: they have committed fornication, and have not ceased: because they have forsaken the Lord in not observing his law.

JPS (1917)

And they shall eat, and not have enough, they shall commit harlotry, and shall not increase; because they have left off to take heed to the LORD.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Et comedent, et non saturabuntur; fornicati sunt, et non cessaverunt: quoniam Dominum dereliquerunt in non custodiendo.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

فَيَأْكُلُونَ وَلَا يَشْبَعُونَ، وَيَزْنُونَ وَلَا يَكْثُرُونَ، لِأَنَّهُمْ قَدْ تَرَكُوا عِبَادَةَ ٱلرَّبِّ.

The Book of Hosea 4:11.

Whoredom and wine and new wine take away the heart.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

זְנ֛וּת וְיַ֥יִן וְתִיר֖וֹשׁ יִֽקַּֽח־לֵֽב׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

πορνείαν. καὶ οἶνον καὶ μέθυσμα ἐδέξατο καρδία λαοῦ μου·

Douay-Rheims

Fornication, and wine, and drunkenness take away the understanding.

JPS (1917)

Harlotry, wine, and new wine take away the heart.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Fornicatio, et vinum, et ebrietas auferunt cor.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

«اَلزِّنَى وَٱلْخَمْرُ وَٱلسُّلَافَةُ تَخْلِبُ ٱلْقَلْبَ.

The Book of Hosea 4:12.

¶ My people ask counsel at their stocks, and their staff declareth unto them: for the spirit of whoredoms hath caused [them] to err, and they have gone a whoring from under their God.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

עַמִּי֙ בְּעֵצ֣וֹ יִשְׁאָ֔ל וּמַקְל֖וֹ יַגִּ֣יד ל֑וֹ כִּ֣י ר֤וּחַ זְנוּנִים֙ הִתְעָ֔ה וַיִּזְנ֖וּ מִתַּ֥חַת אֱלֹהֵיהֶֽם׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ἐν συμβόλοις ἐπηρώτων, καὶ ἐν ῥάβδοις αὐτοῦ ἀπήγγελλον αὐτῷ· πνεύματι πορνείας ἐπλανήθησαν καὶ ἐξεπόρνευσαν ἀπὸ τοῦ θεοῦ αὐτῶν.

Douay-Rheims

My people have consulted their stocks, and their staff hath declared unto them: for the spirit of fornication hath deceived them, and they have committed fornication against their God.

JPS (1917)

My people ask counsel at their stock, and their staff declareth unto them; for the spirit of harlotry hath caused them to err, and they have gone astray from under their God.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Populus meus in ligno suo interrogavit, et baculus ejus annuntiavit ei; spiritus enim fornicationum decepit eos, et fornicati sunt a Deo suo.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

شَعْبِي يَسْأَلُ خَشَبَهُ، وَعَصَاهُ تُخْبِرُهُ، لِأَنَّ رُوحَ ٱلزِّنَى قَدْ أَضَلَّهُمْ فَزَنَوْا مِنْ تَحْتِ إِلَهِهِمْ.

The Book of Hosea 4:13.

They sacrifice upon the tops of the mountains, and burn incense upon the hills, under oaks and poplars and elms, because the shadow thereof [is] good: therefore your daughters shall commit whoredom, and your spouses shall commit adultery.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

עַל־רָאשֵׁ֨י הֶהָרִ֜ים יְזַבֵּ֗חוּ וְעַל־הַגְּבָעוֹת֙ יְקַטֵּ֔רוּ תַּ֣חַת אַלּ֧וֹן וְלִבְנֶ֛ה וְאֵלָ֖ה כִּ֣י ט֣וֹב צִלָּ֑הּ עַל־כֵּ֗ן תִּזְנֶ֨ינָה֙ בְּנ֣וֹתֵיכֶ֔ם וְכַלּוֹתֵיכֶ֖ם תְּנָאַֽפְנָה׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ἐπὶ τὰς κορυφὰς τῶν ὀρέων ἐθυσίαζον, καὶ ἐπὶ τοὺς βουνοὺς ἔθυον ὑποκάτω δρυὸς καὶ λεύκης καὶ δένδρου συσκιάζοντος, ὅτι καλὸν σκέπη. διὰ τοῦτο ἐκπορνεύσουσιν αἱ θυγατέρες ὑμῶν, καὶ αἱ νύμφαι ὑμῶν μοιχεύσουσιν·

Douay-Rheims

They offered sacrifice upon the tops of the mountains, and burnt incense upon the hills: under the oak, and the poplar, and the turpentine tree, because the shadow thereof was good: therefore shall your daughters commit fornication, and your spouses shall be adulteresses.

JPS (1917)

They sacrifice upon the tops of the mountains, and offer upon the hills, under oaks and poplars and terebinths, because the shadow thereof is good; therefore your daughters commit harlotry, and your daughters-in-law commit adultery.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Super capita montium sacrificabant, et super colles ascendebant thymiama; subtus quercum, et populum, et terebinthum, quia bona erat umbra ejus; ideo fornicabuntur filiæ vestræ, et sponsæ vestræ adulteræ erunt.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

يَذْبَحُونَ عَلَى رُؤُوسِ ٱلْجِبَالِ، وَيُبَخِّرُونَ عَلَى ٱلتِّلَالِ تَحْتَ ٱلْبَلُّوطِ وَٱللُّبْنَى وَٱلْبُطْمِ لِأَنَّ ظِلَّهَا حَسَنٌ! لِذَلِكَ تَزْنِي بَنَاتُكُمْ وَتَفْسِقُ كَنَّاتُكُمْ.

↩

The Book of Hosea 4:14.

I will not punish your daughters when they commit whoredom, nor your spouses when they commit adultery: for themselves are separated with whores, and they sacrifice with harlots: therefore the people [that] doth not understand shall fall.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

לֹֽא־אֶפְק֨וֹד עַל־בְּנוֹתֵיכֶ֜ם כִּ֣י תִזְנֶ֗ינָה וְעַל־כַּלּֽוֹתֵיכֶם֙ כִּ֣י תְנָאַ֔פְנָה כִּי־הֵם֙ עִם־הַזֹּנ֣וֹת יְפָרֵ֔דוּ וְעִם־הַקְּדֵשׁ֖וֹת יְזַבֵּ֑חוּ וְעָ֥ם לֹֽא־יָבִ֖ין יִלָּבֵֽט׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

καὶ οὐ μὴ ἐπισκέψωμαι ἐπὶ τὰς θυγατέρας ὑμῶν ὅταν πορνεύωσιν, καὶ ἐπὶ τὰς νύμφας ὑμῶν ὅταν μοιχεύωσιν· ὅτι αὐτοὶ μετὰ τῶν πορνῶν συνεφύροντο, καὶ μετὰ τῶν τετελεσμένων ἔθυον, καὶ ὁ λαὸς ὁ συνίων συνεπλέκετο μετὰ πόρνης.

Douay-Rheims

I will not visit upon your daughters when they shell commit fornication, and upon your spouses when they shall commit adultery: because themselves conversed with harlots, and offered sacrifice with the effeminate, and the people that doth not understand shall be beaten.

JPS (1917)

I will not punish your daughters when they commit harlotry, nor your daughters-in-law when they commit adultery; for they themselves consort with lewd women, and they sacrifice with harlots; and the people that is without understanding is distraught.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Non visitabo super filias vestras cum fuerint fornicatæ, et super sponsas vestras cum adulteraverint, quoniam ipsi cum meretricibus conversabantur, et cum effeminatis sacrificabant; et populus non intelligens vapulabit.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

لَا أُعَاقِبُ بَنَاتِكُمْ لِأَنَّهُنَّ يَزْنِينَ، وَلَا كَنَّاتِكُمْ لِأَنَّهُنَّ يَفْسِقْنَ. لِأَنَّهُمْ يَعْتَزِلُونَ مَعَ ٱلزَّانِيَاتِ وَيَذْبَحُونَ مَعَ ٱلنَّاذِرَاتِ ٱلزِّنَى. وَشَعْبٌ لَا يَعْقِلُ يُصْرَعُ.

↩

The Book of Hosea 4:15.

¶ Though thou, Israel, play the harlot, [yet] let not Judah offend; and come not ye unto Gilgal, neither go ye up to Beth-aven, nor swear, The LORD liveth.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

אִם־זֹנֶ֤ה אַתָּה֙ יִשְׂרָאֵ֔ל אַל־יֶאְשַׁ֖ם יְהוּדָ֑ה וְאַל־תָּבֹ֣אוּ הַגִּלְגָּ֗ל וְאַֽל־תַּעֲלוּ֙ בֵּ֣ית אָ֔וֶן וְאַל־תִּשָּׁבְע֖וּ חַי־יְהוָֽה׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

σὺ δέ, Ἰσραήλ, μὴ ἀγνόει, καὶ Ἰούδα, μὴ εἰσπορεύεσθε εἰς Γάλγαλα, καὶ μὴ ἀναβαίνετε εἰς τὸν οἶκον Ὤν, καὶ μὴ ὀμνύετε ζῶντα Κύριον.

Douay-Rheims

If thou play the harlot, O Israel, at least let not Juda offend: and go ye not into Galgal, and come not up into Bethaven, and do not swear: The Lord liveth.

JPS (1917)

Though thou, Israel, play the harlot, yet let not Judah become guilty; and come not ye unto Gilgal, neither go ye up to Beth-aven, nor swear: 'As the LORD liveth.'

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Si fornicaris tu, Israël, non delinquat saltem Juda; et nolite ingredi in Galgala, et ne ascenderitis in Bethaven, neque juraveritis: Vivit Dominus !

Arabic (Van Dyck)

«إِنْ كُنْتَ أَنْتَ زَانِيًا يَا إِسْرَائِيلُ فَلَا يَأْثَمُ يَهُوذَا. وَلَا تَأْتُوا إِلَى ٱلْجِلْجَالِ وَلَا تَصْعَدُوا إِلَى بَيْتِ آوَنَ وَلَا تَحْلِفُوا: حَيٌّ هُوَ ٱلرَّبُّ.

The Book of Hosea 4:16.

For Israel slideth back as a backsliding heifer: now the LORD will feed them as a lamb in a large place.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

כִּ֚י כְּפָרָ֣ה סֹֽרֵרָ֔ה סָרַ֖ר יִשְׂרָאֵ֑ל עַתָּה֙ יִרְעֵ֣ם יְהוָ֔ה כְּכֶ֖בֶשׂ בַּמֶּרְחָֽב׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

διότι ὡς δάμαλις παροιστρῶσα παροίστρησεν Ἰσραήλ· νῦν νεμήσει αὐτοὺς Κύριος ὡς ἀμνὸν ἐν εὐρυχώρῳ.

Douay-Rheims

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Arabic (Van Dyck)

The Book of Hosea 4:18.

Their drink is sour: they have committed whoredom continually: her rulers [with] shame do love, Give ye.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

סָ֖ר סָבְאָ֑ם הַזְנֵ֣ה הִזְנ֔וּ אָהֲב֥וּ הֵב֛וּ קָל֖וֹן מָגִנֶּֽיהָ׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

ᾑρέτισεν Χαναναίους· πορνεύοντες ἐξεπόρνευσαν, ἠγάπησαν ἀτιμίαν ἐκ φρυάγματος αὐτῆς.

Douay-Rheims

Their banquet is separated, they have gone astray by fornication: they that should have protected them have loved to bring shame upon them.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Arabic (Van Dyck)

The Book of Hosea 4:19.

The wind hath bound her up in her wings, and they shall be ashamed because of their sacrifices.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

צָרַ֥ר ר֛וּחַ אוֹתָ֖הּ בִּכְנָפֶ֑יהָ וְיֵבֹ֖שׁוּ מִזִּבְחוֹתָֽם

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

συστροφὴ πνεύματος σὺ εἶ ἐν ταῖς πτέρυξιν αὐτῆς, καὶ καταισχυνθήσονται ἐκ τῶν θυσιαστηρίων αὐτῶν.

Douay-Rheims

The wind hath bound them up in its wings, and they shall be confounded because of their sacrifices.

JPS (1917)

The wind hath bound her up in her skirts; and they shall be ashamed because of their sacrifices.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

Ligavit eum spiritus in alis suis, et confundentur a sacrificiis suis.]

Arabic (Van Dyck)

قَدْ صَرَّتْهَا ٱلرِّيحُ فِي أَجْنِحَتِهَا، وَخَجِلُوا مِنْ ذَبَائِحِهِمْ.

The Book of Hosea 5:1.

Hear ye this, O priests; and hearken, ye house of Israel; and give ye ear, O house of the king; for judgment [is] toward you, because ye have been a snare on Mizpah, and a net spread upon Tabor.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

שִׁמְעוּ־זֹ֨את הַכֹּהֲנִ֜ים וְהַקְשִׁ֣יבוּ ׀ בֵּ֣ית יִשְׂרָאֵ֗ל וּבֵ֤ית הַמֶּ֨לֶךְ֙ הַאֲזִ֔ינוּ כִּ֥י לָכֶ֖ם הַמִּשְׁפָּ֑ט כִּֽי־פַח֙ הֱיִיתֶ֣ם לְמִצְפָּ֔ה וְרֶ֖שֶׁת פְּרוּשָׂ֥ה עַל־תָּבֽוֹר׃

Greek (Septuagint / Swete)

Ἀκούσατε ταῦτα, οἱ ἱερεῖς, καὶ προσέχετε, οἶκος Ἰσραήλ, καὶ ὁ οἶκος τοῦ βασιλέως, ἐνωτίζεσθε, διότι πρὸς ὑμᾶς ἐστιν τὸ κρίμα· ὅτι παγὶς ἐγενήθητε τῇ σκοπιᾷ, καὶ ὡς δίκτυον ἐκτεταμένον ἐπὶ τὸ Ἰταβύριον,

Douay-Rheims

Hear ye this, O priests, and hearken, O ye house of Israel, and give ear, O house of the king: for there is a judgment against you, because you have been a snare to them whom you should have watched over, and a net spread upon Thabor.

JPS (1917)

Hear this, O ye priests, and attend, ye house of Israel, and give ear, O house of the king, for unto you pertaineth the judgment; for ye have been a snare on Mizpah, and a net spread upon Tabor.

Latin (Clementine Vulgate)

[Audite hoc, sacerdotes, et attendite, domus Israël, et domus regis, auscultate: quia vobis judicium est, quoniam laqueus facti estis speculationi, et rete expansum super Thabor.

Arabic (Van Dyck)

«اِسْمَعُوا هَذَا أَيُّهَا ٱلْكَهَنَةُ! وَٱنْصِتُوا يَا بَيْتَ إِسْرَائِيلَ! وَأَصْغُوا يَا بَيْتَ ٱلْمَلِكِ! لِأَنَّ عَلَيْكُمُ ٱلْقَضَاءَ، إِذْ صِرْتُمْ فَخًّا فِي مِصْفَاةَ، وَشَبَكَةً مَبْسُوطَةً عَلَى تَابُورَ.

Hebrew (Masoretic / WLC)

JPS (1917)
